    Tender Note:
    The bidder shall submit valid documentary evidence, certificates, declarations, compliance reports, and OEM
    datasheets supporting all the specified technical features, protocols, certifications, and compliance requirements.
    Self-declarations without supporting documentary evidence shall not be considered for technical evaluation. Any
    certification under renewal shall be supported by valid proof issued by the respective certification authority. The
    offered camera shall support all specified protocols and standards required for seamless integration with ICCC
    infrastructure, Enterprise Video Management Systems (VMS), Network Management Systems (NMS), and third-
    party ONVIF-compliant platforms. The bidder shall clearly highlight compliance in the OEM datasheets. Any
    feature, protocol, analytics, or functionality requiring additional licenses shall be supplied and included in the
    quoted price without any additional cost to VMC.

    Network Connectivity
    Network connectivity is one of the most important components of the project and needs very careful attention in
    assessment, planning and implementation. It is important not only to ensure that the required connectivity is
    provisioned within the required timelines but also ensure that it is reliable, secure and supports the required SLA
    parameters of Latency, Jitter, Packet Loss and Performance. Throughout end to end (data center till edge level)
    network should support the software defined networking (SDN).

    STQC/BIS-ER
    STQC/BIS-ER Compliance for CCTV Cameras- All new CCTV cameras proposed under this contract, including
    replacement and additional cameras, shall comply with the latest applicable STQC or BIS Essential Requirements
    (BIS-ER) notified by the Government of India. The bidder shall ensure that all supplied CCTV cameras possess
    valid STQC certification and BIS registration, wherever applicable, at the time of supply and installation.
    Documentary evidence, including valid certificates and test reports, shall be submitted to VMC prior to
    deployment. Any camera found non-compliant with STQC/BIS-ER requirements shall be replaced by the bidder
    at no additional cost to VMC.

    Single OEM - Make for All Camera Types
    All CCTV cameras supplied under this tender Bullet Camera, Dome Camera, and PTZ Camera shall be of the
    same OEM (Original Equipment Manufacturer) and same product make/series across the entire project. Mixing
    of cameras from different manufacturers/brands for different camera types (e.g., Bullet from one OEM and
    Dome/PTZ from another) shall not be permitted under any circumstances.

    This requirement is mandatory to ensure:
   Uniform build quality, firmware ecosystem, and cybersecurity posture across all deployed cameras;
   Single-point OEM warranty, support, and spares availability for the entire camera fleet, avoiding multi-vendor
    coordination issues during the O&M period.
   Seamless interoperability, firmware/security patch management, and long-term supportability through a single
    OEM relationship.
   Simplified troubleshooting, AMC/CMC accountability, and reduced integration risk with the central VMS.

    The bidder shall clearly declare, in the technical bid, the single OEM/brand proposed for all three camera types
    (Bullet, Dome, and PTZ), along with the respective model numbers and datasheets. Bids proposing cameras of
    different makes/brands for different camera categories shall be treated as non-compliant and liable for rejection at
    the technical evaluation stage.
    Proposed OEM/make shall hold valid STQC/BIS-ER certification for all three camera models (Bullet, Dome, and
    PTZ) offered under this tender.

    Security Management
    The City Surveillance System shall be designed, implemented, and maintained with comprehensive security
    controls to ensure confidentiality, integrity, availability, and protection of all system components, applications,
    databases, networks, servers, storage systems, CCTV cameras, edge devices, and communication infrastructure.
    The system shall be protected against both internal and external cyber threats, unauthorized access, malware,
    ransomware, denial-of-service attacks, data breaches, and other security vulnerabilities.

                                                                                                                          51

      The Security Management framework shall include, but not be limited to, the following:
     The entire system shall be protected through enterprise-grade anti-virus, anti-malware, anti-ransomware, endpoint
      protection, and security monitoring solutions.
     Appropriate security policies, access controls, authentication mechanisms, audit trails, logging, monitoring, and
      incident response procedures shall be implemented and enforced throughout the contract period.
     All communication between system components shall be secured using industry-standard encryption protocols.
     Role-Based Access Control (RBAC) shall be implemented to ensure access is granted only to authorized users
      based on their roles and responsibilities.
     The system shall support secure password policies, account lockout mechanisms, audit logging, and periodic
      security reviews.
     All servers, storage systems, databases, operating systems, applications, and network devices shall be hardened as
      per industry best practices and OEM recommendations.
     The bidder shall ensure timely deployment of security patches, firmware updates, and vulnerability remediation
      throughout the contract period.
     Processing, recording, analytics, storage, and management of CCTV video feeds shall be hosted only within the
      infrastructure deployed under this project. No CCTV video stream, metadata, analytics data, or recording shall be
      processed or stored on any public cloud platform or on servers located outside Vadodara City without prior written
      approval of VMC.
     The bidder shall maintain detailed security logs and audit trails for all user activities, system events, and
      administrative actions.

Network Connectivity Scope of work
     Network connectivity shall be one of the critical components of the City Surveillance System and shall be
     designed, supplied, implemented, tested, commissioned, operated, and maintained by the bidder. The bidder
     shall conduct detailed site surveys and network planning to ensure seamless end-to-end connectivity between
     field devices, edge devices, aggregation points, network infrastructure, and the Central Command & Control
     Center (CCC).

      The network infrastructure shall be designed to provide reliable, secure, scalable, and high-availability
      communication for CCTV cameras, video analytics, edge devices, switches, servers, storage systems, video
      walls, public address systems, emergency call boxes, sensors, and all associated field equipment. The bidder
      shall ensure that the network meets the prescribed Service Level Agreements (SLAs) related to availability,
      latency, jitter, packet loss, throughput, and overall network performance throughout the contract period.

      The complete network architecture from the Data Center/CCC up to the field level shall support Software
      Defined Networking (SDN) or equivalent technology to enable centralized management, traffic engineering,
      network segmentation, security policy enforcement, automated provisioning, and future scalability.

     The bidder shall be responsible for provisioning all required network components, including fiber optic
     connectivity, switches, routers, media converters, network management software, accessories, and associated
     passive infrastructure required for successful implementation and operation of the project. The network
     design shall ensure redundancy at critical locations to minimize service disruption due to hardware, fiber, or
     communication failures.
Network Monitoring System
     The bidder shall provide, install, configure, commission, and maintain an Enterprise-Class Network
     Monitoring System (NMS) for centralized monitoring and management of the complete surveillance network
     infrastructure. The proposed solution shall provide fault management, performance management,
     configuration monitoring, event correlation, reporting, alerting, and SLA monitoring capabilities.

      The Network Monitoring System shall support monitoring and management of all IP/SNMP-enabled devices
      deployed under the project, including but not limited to CCTV Cameras, Edge Devices, Industrial Switches,
      Routers, Firewalls, Servers, Storage Systems, Video Management Systems (VMS), Video Walls, Public
      Address Systems, Emergency Call Boxes, Sensors, UPS Systems, and other network-connected equipment.

      The Network Monitoring System shall provide the following minimum functionalities:
           a. The system shall automatically discover all manageable network devices and infrastructure
              elements connected to the network and maintain an updated inventory database. The solution shall
              provide a centralized monitoring dashboard with graphical network topology maps displaying

                                                                                                                      58

                 connectivity, device status, and network health.
            b.   The solution shall support configurable dashboards and customizable reporting tools for generation
                 of operational, performance, availability, utilization, fault, inventory, and SLA reports.
            c.   The system shall continuously monitor network bandwidth utilization, link health, packet loss,
                 latency, jitter, uptime, and device availability and shall generate reports for user-defined periods.
            d.   The system shall provide intelligent fault management through event correlation, root cause
                 analysis, dependency mapping, and policy-based fault detection mechanisms to identify
                 infrastructure failures, communication issues, configuration changes, and potential cyber-security
                 incidents.
            e.   The system shall generate real-time alerts and notifications through Dashboard, Email, SMS,
                 Mobile Application, or other approved notification mechanisms whenever any critical event, fault,
                 device failure, communication loss, security incident, threshold breach, or configuration change is
                 detected.
            f.   The system shall maintain audit logs of all configuration changes, user activities, network events,
                 and administrative actions for compliance and troubleshooting purposes.
            g.   The system shall support SLA monitoring and reporting for all critical infrastructure components
                 and generate automated monthly availability and performance reports.
            h.   The solution shall support northbound integration with the proposed ICCC platform, Video
                 Management System (VMS), Helpdesk/Ticketing System, and other third-party systems through
                 standard APIs and protocols.
            i.   The Network Monitoring System shall support role-based access control (RBAC), secure
                 authentication, encrypted communication, and audit logging in accordance with prevailing cyber
                 security guidelines and industry best practices.
            j.   All required software licenses, dashboards, reports, integrations, database licenses, upgrades,
                 patches, and support services for the Network Monitoring System shall be included by the bidder
                 for the entire contract period.
Non-IT and Civil Infrastructure Specifications & Guidelines
1. Public Works Guidelines
      The Bidder/System Integrator (SI) shall be responsible for obtaining all necessary permissions and carrying
      out all civil, electrical, and public infrastructure works required under the project. The SI shall comply with
      all applicable regulations, standards, and instructions issued by Vadodara Municipal Corporation (VMC),
      Vadodara City Police, Road & Building Department, Utility Agencies, and other statutory authorities.
      The scope of public works shall include, but not be limited to, excavation, trenching, road cutting, pole
      foundation construction, pole erection, junction box installation, cable laying, OFC laying, restoration work,
      wiring, barricading, fencing, signage, labeling, safety markings, and all associated civil and electrical works.
      The SI shall restore all roads, footpaths, pavements, and public properties to their original condition after
      completion of work.

2. PVC Conduit and Cable Protection
      All conduits, ducts, HDPE pipes, PVC pipes, cable protection systems, and associated accessories shall
      conform to relevant BIS/ISI standards and industry best practices.
      PVC conduits used for power and communication cabling shall be heavy-duty, UV-resistant, fire-retardant,
      corrosion-resistant, and suitable for outdoor installations. All materials shall carry valid ISI/BIS certification
      wherever applicable.
      The SI shall ensure adequate protection of all cables against mechanical damage, water ingress, rodents,
      environmental exposure, and unauthorized access.

3. Earthing and Lightning Protection
      The SI shall provide complete earthling and bonding arrangements for all electrical and electronic equipment
      deployed under the project, including CCTV cameras, poles, junction boxes, switches, UPS systems, cabinets,
      servers, storage systems, and communication equipment.
      All earthing works shall comply with the latest version of IS 3043 (Code of Practice for Earthing) and other
      applicable Indian Standards.

                                                                                                                           59

      The SI shall ensure that all metallic structures and equipment likely to become energized are effectively
      earthed. Surge protection devices and lightning protection mechanisms shall be provided wherever required to
      safeguard field and central infrastructure.
      No separate payment shall be made for earthing and related accessories, and the same shall be deemed
      included in the quoted price.

4. Passive Cabling and Structured Cabling
       The SI shall provide complete passive cabling infrastructure required for successful operation of the project.
       All structured cabling shall comply with applicable ISO/IEC, TIA/EIA, and BIS standards. The cabling
       solution shall support data, video, voice, network management, and future expansion requirements.
       The SI shall use suitable categories of network cables, fiber optic cables, patch panels, connectors, modular
       sockets, termination hardware, cable trays, ducts, and accessories as per project requirements.
       All cables shall be properly routed, labeled, terminated, documented, and tested. Cable layouts shall be neat,
       organized, and easily maintainable. Adequate spare capacity shall be provided for future scalability and
       expansion.

5. Electrical Wiring Guidelines
       All electrical wiring shall be carried out using ISI-marked copper conductor cables conforming to relevant
       BIS and IEC standards.
       The wiring system shall comply with the following minimum requirements:
          PVC insulated stranded copper conductor cables of appropriate rating shall be used for all electrical
             installations.
          Looping system of wiring shall be adopted. Direct twisting or unauthorized joints in conductors shall
             not be permitted.
          All cables, conductors, and circuits shall be properly identified using ferrules, labels, tags, and cable
             markers at both ends.
          Power cables, communication cables, and fiber optic cables shall be routed separately to avoid
             electromagnetic interference and facilitate maintenance.
          Circuits originating from different power sources, distribution boards, or phases shall not be routed
             through the same conduit unless specifically approved.
          All cable terminations and splicing shall be carried out using approved terminal blocks, connectors,
             glands, and accessories. Twisted wire joints shall not be permitted.
          All sockets, switches, distribution boards, junction boxes, and enclosures shall be industrial-grade and
             suitable for outdoor deployment wherever applicable.
          Proper phase balancing shall be ensured for three-phase electrical installations.
          All electrical installations shall comply with applicable Indian Electricity Rules, Central Electricity
             Authority (CEA) Regulations, National Electrical Code (NEC), BIS Standards, and IEC Standards.
          The SI shall be responsible for supply, installation, testing, commissioning, documentation,
             certification, and maintenance of all electrical infrastructure deployed under the project.

6. Quality, Safety and Compliance
         The SI shall follow all applicable safety regulations during execution of work. Necessary barricading,
             caution boards, warning signs, traffic diversion arrangements, personal protective equipment (PPE),
             and worksite safety measures shall be provided throughout the project duration.
      All materials supplied under the project shall be new, genuine, and sourced from authorized manufacturers.
      The SI shall submit test certificates, compliance certificates, warranty certificates, and inspection reports
      whenever requested by VMC.
      All civil, electrical, and non-IT infrastructure works shall be executed in a professional manner and shall
      comply with applicable BIS, IEC, IS, IEEE, TIA/EIA, ISO, and other relevant national and international
      standards.
     Pole, Foundation & Structural Stability Certificate
      The Successful Bidder shall design, supply, install, and certify all CCTV poles, mounting structures, brackets,
      and foundations for structural adequacy and stability in accordance with the applicable Indian Standards and

                                                                                                                        60

      good engineering practices. A Structural Stability Certificate, duly signed and stamped by a
      Chartered/Qualified Structural Engineer, shall be submitted to VMC after installation and before Go-Live.
      The Structural Stability Certificate shall remain valid for the entire five (5) year Contract Period after Go-
      Live. In case the certificate issued by the Structural Engineer is valid for a shorter period (e.g., three (3)
      years), the Successful Bidder shall, at its own cost, obtain and submit a renewed/revalidated Structural
      Stability Certificate before expiry of the earlier certificate so that the structural stability certification remains
      valid continuously throughout the entire Contract Period. Any failure, tilting, settlement, corrosion, structural
      damage, or instability of the pole or foundation shall be rectified or replaced by the Successful Bidder at no
      additional cost to VMC.

       Standardized Signage for CCTV Surveillance Locations
      In order to ensure transparency, public awareness, and compliance with applicable privacy and surveillance
      regulations, the Bidder/System Integrator (SI) shall provide, install, and maintain standardized CCTV
      surveillance signage at all camera locations covered under the project.
      The purpose of the signage shall be to clearly inform citizens, visitors, and stakeholders that the area is under
      video surveillance and that activities within the coverage area may be monitored and recorded for public
      safety, security, traffic management, law enforcement, and incident investigation purposes.
      The signage shall be prominently displayed at visible locations near CCTV installations, entry points, public
      access areas, intersections, public buildings, parks, gardens, swimming pools, town halls, crematoriums,
      municipal offices, sensitive locations, and other surveillance zones identified by VMC. The location, size,
      design, and placement of signage shall ensure maximum visibility to the public without obstructing pedestrian
      or vehicular movement.
      The SI shall design, manufacture, supply, install, and maintain weatherproof, UV-resistant, vandal-resistant,
      and corrosion-resistant signage suitable for outdoor environmental conditions. The signage shall include
      appropriate pictorial symbols indicating CCTV surveillance along with messages in Gujarati, Hindi, and
      English languages as approved by VMC.
      The signage shall contain, at a minimum, the following information:
      "YOU ARE UNDER CCTV SURVEILLANCE"
      "This area is monitored and recorded through CCTV Cameras for public safety, security, traffic management,
      and law enforcement purposes."
      "Vadodara Municipal Corporation (VMC)"
      The design, content, dimensions, material specifications, language, color scheme, reflectivity requirements,
      and installation methodology shall be submitted by the bidder for approval of VMC before implementation.
      The bidder shall be responsible for replacement of damaged, faded, stolen, defaced, or missing signboards
      throughout the warranty and maintenance period at no additional cost to VMC.
      All costs related to design, manufacturing, transportation, installation, maintenance, replacement, fixtures,
      clamps, mounting arrangements, permissions, and associated works shall be deemed included in the bidder's
      quoted price and no separate payment shall be made by VMC.

Earthing
                                                       Minimum Specifications
      All electrical components are to be earthen by connecting two earth tapes from the frame of the component ring and will be
      connected via several earth electrodes. The cable arm will be earthen through the cable glands. Earthling shall be in
      conformity with provision of rules 32, 61, 62, 67 & 68 of Indian Electricity rules 1956 and as per IS-3043.
      Earthing should be done for the entire power system and provisioning should be there to earth UPS systems, Power
      distribution units etc. so as to avoid a ground differential. Authority shall provide the necessary space required to prepare
      the earthing pits.
      All metallic objects on the premises that are likely to be energized by electric currents should be effectively grounded.
      The connection to the earth or the electrode system should have sufficient low resistance in the range of 0 to 25 ohm to
      ensure prompt operation of respective protective devices in event of a ground fault, to provide the required safety from an
      electric shock to personnel & protect the equipment from voltage gradients which are likely to damage the equipment.
      Recommended levels for equipment grounding conductors should have very low impedance level less than 0.25 ohm.
      The Earth resistance shall be automatically measured on an online basis at a pre-configured interval and corrective action
      should be initiated based on the observation. The automatic Earthing measurements should be available on the UPS panel
      itself in the UPS.
      There should be enough space between data and power cabling and there should not be any cross wiring of the two, in
      order to avoid any interference, or corruption of data.
      The earth connections shall be properly made .A small copper loop to bridge the top cover of the transformer and the tank
      shall be provided to avoid earth fault current passing through fastened bolts, when there is a lighting surge, high voltage
      surge or failure of bushings.

  The bidder shall be responsible for the design, supply, installation, integration, testing, commissioning,
   operation, maintenance, warranty support, and successful functioning of all hardware, software, licenses,
   accessories, passive components, electrical infrastructure, networking components, and associated services
   required for implementation of the City Surveillance System. The technical specifications mentioned in this
   RFP for UPS Systems, Surge Protection Devices, Earthing Systems, Camera Mounting Poles, Junction Boxes,
   Tape Data Cartridges, Video Management Software (VMS), SAN Switches, Top-of-Rack Switches, Indoor
   Active LED Display Video Walls, Video Wall Controllers, EMS/NMS, and all other related equipment shall be
   considered as the minimum acceptable specifications.
  The bidder shall ensure that all proposed components are enterprise-grade, commercially available, currently
   supported by the OEM, and suitable for continuous 24x7x365 mission-critical surveillance operations. All
   equipment supplied under the project shall be new, unused, and sourced through authorized channels. The
   bidder shall ensure complete interoperability and seamless integration among all proposed components,
   irrespective of the OEMs involved.
  Any component, accessory, software license, mounting arrangement, cable, connector, patch cord, rack
   accessory, power distribution unit, surge protection device, earthing material, management software,
   monitoring tool, or any other item required for successful implementation and operation of the project, whether
   specifically mentioned in the Bill of Quantities (BoQ) or not, shall be deemed to be included within the bidder's
   scope without any additional cost to VMC.
  The bidder may offer specifications higher than those prescribed in this RFP; however, under no circumstances
   shall specifications lower than those specified be accepted. All proposed products shall comply with applicable
   BIS, IEC, ISO, IEEE, ONVIF, UL, CE, FCC, RoHS, NDAA, and other relevant national and international
   standards wherever applicable. The bidder shall submit OEM datasheets, technical literature, compliance
   statements, certifications, and test reports supporting compliance with the specified requirements.
  The bidder shall provide all necessary software licenses, firmware, subscriptions, updates, patches, security
   upgrades, management tools, and integration services required for successful operation of the system
   throughout the contract period. The complete solution shall be scalable and capable of supporting future
   expansion without requiring replacement of existing infrastructure.
  All equipment supplied under the project shall be covered under comprehensive OEM warranty and support for
   the period specified in the RFP. During the warranty and maintenance period, the bidder shall be responsible
   for replacement, repair, software updates, security patches, configuration support, and restoration of services to
   ensure compliance with the prescribed Service Level Agreements (SLAs).
  The successful bidder shall be solely responsible for the performance, reliability, availability, security,
   maintainability, and operational readiness of the complete City Surveillance System and all associated
   infrastructure deployed under the project.
  The proposed solution shall be based on open architecture and open industry standards to ensure
   interoperability, scalability, and future expansion.
  All hardware, software, licenses, accessories, tools, consumables, and services required for successful
   implementation of the project shall be deemed to be included in the Bid.
  The proposed solution shall be compatible with the existing ICT infrastructure, surveillance systems, network,
   servers, storage, security solutions, and third-party applications available at VMC.
  The solution shall support integration through standard APIs, ONVIF, SNMP, REST APIs, Syslog,
   LDAP/Active Directory, TCP/IP, HTTPS, and other industry-standard protocols wherever applicable.
  All integration, customization, configuration, migration, testing, commissioning, and interoperability activities
   required for successful implementation shall be carried out by the Successful Bidder without any additional
   cost to VMC.

   replacement of the core infrastructure.

      5.3    CAPEX Implementation Delay Penalty
       The Successful Bidder shall complete the entire CAPEX scope, including supply, installation, integration, testing,
       commissioning, documentation, training, and Go-Live of the complete City Surveillance System within the
       stipulated timeline specified in the Approved Project Schedule and in any case not later than the final Go-Live date
       specified in the Work Order.

       In the event of delay attributable to the Bidder, the following penalties shall be applicable:

                     Delay Period                                  Penalty
                     Up to 15 Days        0.5% of total CAPEX Contract Value per week or part thereof
                     16 to 30 Days        1.0% of total CAPEX Contract Value per week or part thereof
                     31 to 60 Days        1.5% of total CAPEX Contract Value per week or part thereof
                     Beyond 60 Days       2.0% of total CAPEX Contract Value per week or part thereof

       The maximum cumulative penalty for delay in completion of CAPEX implementation shall be limited to 10% of the
       Total CAPEX Contract Value.

       For milestone-based monitoring, VMC may also impose penalties for non-achievement of approved milestones. The
       penalty shall be calculated as follows:

       Penalty = (Value of Delayed Milestone × Applicable Penalty Percentage × Number of Weeks Delayed)

       If the delay in completion exceeds 90 days from the approved Go-Live date, VMC reserves the right to:

              Invoke Performance Bank Guarantee (PBG).
              Procure remaining work through an alternative agency at the risk and cost of the Bidder.
              Recover additional costs incurred by VMC from pending payments and/or security deposits.
              Terminate the contract after giving due notice.

       No penalty shall be applicable for delays arising due to force majeure events, written instructions from VMC,
       statutory approval delays not attributable to the Bidder, or any other reasons expressly approved by VMC in writing.

       Illustration:
          Total CAPEX Contract Value = INR 5 Crore
          Delay = 4 Weeks

                                                                                                                            85

    Applicable Penalty = 1% per week
    Penalty = INR5 Crore × 1% × 4 = INR 20 Lakh

However, the total penalty shall not exceed 10% of the CAPEX Contract Value, i.e., INR 5 Crore.

Note: Penalty for implementation delay shall be independent of SLA penalties applicable during the OPEX period.
Both penalties may be levied separately wherever applicable.

5.4   Site Survey and BOQ Verification
      The Successful Bidder shall carry out a detailed site survey of all locations provided by VMC within
      fifteen (15) days from the date of handover of the locations by VMC and submit the Site Survey
      Report for VMC's review and approval.
      Detailed Site Survey and Submission of Site Survey Report shall be completed from the date the
      locations are provided by VMC.
      The quantities provided in the tender BOQ are indicative and intended for evaluation purposes. The
      bidder shall verify the actual site requirements, installation conditions, power availability, network
      connectivity, mounting arrangements, civil requirements, and other infrastructure requirements during
      the site survey.
      Based on the approved site survey, the bidder shall submit the location-wise detailed Bill of Quantities
      (BOQ) and implementation plan. Billing shall be made based on the actual quantities supplied,
      installed, tested, commissioned, and accepted by VMC at the approved unit rates quoted in the
      Financial Bid.
      In case any variation in quantity, addition, deletion, or modification of items is identified during the
      site survey, the same shall be brought to the notice of VMC immediately for approval before
      execution. No additional work or quantity variation shall be executed without prior written approval
      from VMC.
      The bidder shall be responsible for completing the project objectives and ensuring full functionality of
      the City Surveillance System. Any minor items, accessories, fittings, mounting hardware, cabling,
      connectors, consumables, software components, or other materials required for successful
      implementation of the project, but not specifically mentioned in the BOQ, shall be deemed included in
      the bidder's scope and quoted rates, and no separate payment shall be made for such items.
      Payment shall be released based on the actual executed and accepted quantities as certified by VMC.
      However, the bidder shall not be entitled to claim any compensation, damages, idle charges, or loss of
      profit on account of variation in quantities from the indicative BOQ provided in the tender.
      VMC reserves the right to increase, decrease, add, delete, or modify the quantities of any item during
      the contract period based on actual site requirements, budget availability, and project needs, and
      payment shall be made at the approved contract unit rates.

5.5   Existing Hardware Assessment and Reuse
      The Successful Bidder shall conduct a detailed site survey and technical assessment of the existing
      hardware, infrastructure, and associated equipment available at VMC before execution of the work.
      Wherever technically feasible and approved by VMC, the existing hardware shall be reused and
      integrated into the proposed solution.
      The BOQ provided in this Tender is indicative. The final quantity of new equipment shall be finalized
      based on the joint site survey, assessment of reusable existing hardware, and approval of VMC.
      Payment shall be made strictly on the actual quantities supplied, installed, commissioned, and
      accepted, after considering the reuse of existing hardware. The Bidder shall have no claim for
      variation in quantities arising out of such assessment.
      The Successful Bidder shall submit a detailed Existing Hardware Assessment Report, indicating
      reusable, repairable, and non-reusable equipment, along with recommendations and supporting
      photographs, for approval by VMC before procurement and execution of the work.

5.6   Refurbished/Used Equipment
      The Successful Bidder shall supply only new, unused, genuine, and currently manufactured hardware,
      software, and accessories. Refurbished, repaired, reconditioned, remanufactured, second-hand, or End-
      of-Life (EOL)/End-of-Support (EOS) products shall not be permitted under this Contract. The Bidder
      shall submit OEM authorization and warranty/support documents, if required by VMC, as proof of
      compliance.

5.7   Structured Cabling and Cable Management Requirements
      The Successful Bidder shall implement a structured cabling system for all Data Centre, CCC, Network Rooms,
      Rack Locations, Field Cabinets, Junction Boxes, and Surveillance Sites. All cables shall be properly routed,

                                                                                                                 88

      dressed, bundled, tagged, ferruled, and terminated in a neat and professional manner using industry best
      practices.
      The Bidder shall ensure proper cable management through cable trays, raceways, ducts, cable managers, patch
      panels, and rack organizers. Loose, hanging, exposed, improperly terminated, or unlabelled cables shall not be
      acceptable. All power, network, fiber, and control cables shall be clearly identified at both ends with
      permanent labels indicating source and destination details.
      All racks, cabinets, patch panels, switches, servers, and field equipment shall be properly organized and
      documented to facilitate maintenance, troubleshooting, and future expansion. The Bidder shall submit cable
      schedules, labelling details, rack layouts, and as-built documentation for approval by VMC. Any untidy,
      unsafe, or non-standard cabling installation observed during inspection shall be rectified by the Bidder at no
      additional cost to VMC.

   The minimum trench depth for laying Optical Fiber Cable (OFC) shall be 1.65 meter from the
    finished ground level and near ground level. Depending on site conditions, road crossings, utility
    crossings, terrain, statutory requirements, or directions of VMC/concerned authorities, the trench
    depth shall be increased wherever required. Under no circumstances shall the trench depth be less than
    1.6 meters, unless otherwise specifically approved in writing by VMC.
   All LT power/electrical cables from the CCTV pole to the Junction Box (JB) shall be laid at a
    minimum trench depth of 0.8 meters from the finished ground level. Wherever required due to site
    conditions, road crossings, utility crossings, or statutory requirements, the trench depth shall be
    increased up to 1.0 meter or more. Suitable protection such as DWC/HDPE pipe, GI pipe, or
    RCC/concrete encasement shall be provided wherever the minimum depth cannot be achieved or as
    directed by VMC.
   Where the required trench depth cannot be achieved due to site constraints, the cables shall be
    protected using HDPE/DWC pipes, GI pipes, RCC/concrete encasement, or any other approved
    protection method, as directed by VMC.
   After completion of cable laying, the trench shall be backfilled, compacted, and the
    road/footpath/pavement restored to its original condition or better, to the satisfaction of VMC and the
    concerned authority.

5.11 Asset Management Services:
     The Successful Bidder shall establish, maintain, and update a comprehensive Asset Management
     System for all hardware, software, licenses, networking components, field infrastructure, and
     associated assets deployed under this project throughout the contract period.
     The scope of Asset Management Services shall include, but not be limited to, the following:
         o   Maintaining a centralized inventory database of all project assets including CCTV Cameras,
             PTZ Cameras, Edge Devices, Poles, Junction Boxes, Industrial Switches, UPS Systems,
             Sensors, Servers, Storage Systems, Video Wall Components, Networking Equipment, Fiber
             Infrastructure, Software Licenses, and other associated equipment.
         o   Recording detailed information of each asset including OEM Name, Make, Model, Serial
             Number, Asset ID, Configuration Details, Firmware Version, IP Address, Physical Location,
             Installation Date, Warranty Details, License Details, and Operational Status.
         o   Maintaining a complete software and license inventory including software versions, license
             validity, license keys, subscription details, support status, security patches, upgrades, and
             renewal history.
         o   Maintaining records of installation, commissioning, relocation, repair, replacement,
             preventive maintenance, breakdown maintenance, decommissioning, and disposal of all
             project assets.
         o   Recording all entry, exit, transfer, temporary replacement, and movement of assets during the
             contract period and submitting monthly asset inventory and movement reports to VMC.
         o   Assigning a unique Asset Identification Number/Tag to each asset supplied and installed
             under the project.
         o   Maintaining location-wise, asset-wise, OEM-wise, warranty-wise, and license-wise inventory
             records throughout the contract period.
         o   Ensuring 24x7x365 monitoring and availability of all CCTV cameras, poles, edge devices,
             networking equipment, servers, storage systems, video walls, sensors, software applications,
             and associated infrastructure as per the prescribed SLA requirements.
         o   Maintaining complete fault history, maintenance history, replacement history, and health
             status records for each asset.
         o   Providing periodic reports including Asset Inventory Reports, Warranty Reports, License
             Compliance Reports, Preventive Maintenance Reports, Breakdown Reports, and Asset Health
             Reports as and when required by VMC.
         o   Immediately notifying the designated VMC authority regarding any theft, vandalism,
             accidental damage, fire incident, natural calamity, or any other event affecting project assets

                                                                                                               94

              and generating an incident report for the same.
          o   Coordinating with VMC for lodging complaints/FIRs and completing necessary
              documentation in case of theft, vandalism, or loss of equipment.

              timelines in case of equipment failure, damage, or replacement requirements.
          o   Ensuring that all asset records are updated within 24 hours of any installation, replacement,

    (Subject to On Ground Survey).

    Corporation shall have the right to carry out the repair/replacement through a third party, and the
    entire cost incurred along with applicable administrative charges shall be recovered from the
    Contractor’s pending bills, Performance Security, or any other dues payable.
   Payments for services during the warranty period shall be released on a quarterly basis, subject to
    satisfactory performance of the System Integrator/Vendor and compliance with the applicable Service
    Level Agreements (SLAs). The payment shall be made after verification of services rendered during
    the respective quarter and submission of relevant reports, invoices, and supporting documents. Any
    applicable penalties or deductions as per the contract shall be adjusted prior to release of payment.
   The Bidder shall ensure that all CCTV cameras provide clear and usable video footage during both
    day and night conditions, including effective IR/Night Vision performance as per the specified camera
    coverage area. If any incident footage is found unclear, blurred, distorted, overexposed, underexposed,
    or not suitable for identification of persons, vehicles, registration numbers, or incident activities due to
    camera malfunction, IR/Night Vision failure, poor image quality, recording failure, storage failure, or
    network failure attributable to the Bidder, the same shall be treated as a Critical SLA Violation. VMC
    may impose a penalty of ₹5,000 per camera per incident and direct immediate rectification at the
    Bidder's cost.
   The System Integrator/Vendor shall maintain a complete and updated asset register for all supplied
    equipment, including cameras, batteries, routers, all components, and other field devices. Each asset
    shall be uniquely identified and recorded with make, model, serial number, location, and date of
    installation. In the event of any repair, replacement, or new supply, the System Integrator/Vendor
    shall ensure proper documentation and traceability. The details of the old (removed) item, repaired
    item, and new/replacement item shall be recorded with respective serial numbers, date of
    replacement/repair, reason for replacement, and current status. All replaced or faulty equipment shall

                                                                                                                   96

         be tagged and returned/handed over to VMC or stored as per VMC directions. The System
         Integrator/Vendor shall ensure that any replacement equipment provided is new or OEM-certified,
         meeting the same or higher specifications. The complete record of such activities shall be maintained
         and submitted to VMC on a monthly/quarterly basis or as required, ensuring full transparency,
         auditability, and lifecycle tracking of all assets under the project.

 5.13 SLA for Uptime - Availability of CITY SURVEILLANCE SYSTEM Pole,
      camera with Edge Device
During the Operation & Management period the implementing Agency is expected to keep the Technology and
Performance levels above the specified level. It will be the responsibility of the implementing IA to design and
code the software to ensure application is measurable in terms of uptime and performance

Bidder shall deploy and maintain a Ticket-Based Incident Management System for logging, tracking, escalation,
resolution, and closure of all incidents related to the City Surveillance System.

The measurement of service availability and SLA compliance shall be based on:
        Reports generated from the Central Management Software, Video Management System (VMS),
         Network Management System (NMS), Ticketing System software deployed under the project.
        Inspection, verification, and audit conducted by VMC or its authorized representatives.
        Reports generated through licensed monitoring tools deployed by the Bidder.
        Reports generated through monitoring tools, applications, or platforms deployed by the Authority
         (VMC), Independent Agency, or Client during the contract period.

The records and reports generated from the above systems shall form the basis for calculation of uptime,
downtime, incident response time, incident resolution time, and SLA compliance. In case of any dispute, the
records maintained by VMC or its authorized monitoring platform shall prevail and be considered final for SLA
assessment and penalty calculation. Bidder shall ensure continuous availability, operation, maintenance, and
support of all components supplied, installed, integrated, and commissioned under the Project. The SLA
framework is intended to ensure timely fault rectification, system availability, and uninterrupted surveillance
services throughout the Contract Period.

The SLA shall apply to all field and central infrastructure including but not limited to:

        Fixed CCTV Cameras
        PTZ Cameras
        Edge Devices
        Junction Boxes
        Poles and Mounting Structures
        UPS Systems
        Network Switches
        OFC Connectivity
        Servers
        Storage Systems
        Video Management System (VMS)
        Active Video wall
        Recording Services
        Complete Surveillance Nodes

           Total Time = Total hours during the month.
           Downtime = Total period during which the component/service remained unavailable.

5.15 Quarterly SLA Penalty Matrix
 SLA performance shall be monitored monthly; however, penalties shall be accumulated and recovered from the
 Quarterly Invoice.
                  SLA Achievement During Quarter                  Penalty Applicable
                       ≥ SLA Requirement                                   Nil
                      SLA shortfall up to 2%              2% of Quarterly O&M/CAMC Charges
                        SLA shortfall > 4%                4% of Quarterly O&M/CAMC Charges
                        SLA shortfall > 6%                6% of Quarterly O&M/CAMC Charges
                        SLA shortfall > 8%                8% of Quarterly O&M/CAMC Charges
                       SLA shortfall >10%                10% of Quarterly O&M/CAMC Charges

 Component-wise Penalty Calculation Methodology

 For distributed components such as Cameras, Poles, Edge Devices, UPS, Switches, Junction Boxes, and OFC
 Links, penalty shall be calculated proportionately based on the affected quantity.

                                                                                                              98

Example.:
                       Particular           Value
               Quarterly O&M Charges     INR 30,00,000
               Total Cameras Installed        900
               Cameras Not Meeting SLA         18
               Applicable Penalty             2%

Central Infrastructure Penalty

The Bidder shall ensure continuous availability and smooth operation of all centralized infrastructure
components, including but not limited to the Video Management System (VMS), Recording Servers,
Application Servers, Database Servers, Storage Systems, Central Monitoring Software, Active Video Wall,
Video Wall Controllers, and associated software and hardware components. Since these systems are critical and
support the overall surveillance ecosystem, any failure or unavailability of such centralized infrastructure shall
be considered a major service disruption impacting the entire project.

The availability of centralized infrastructure shall be measured on a 24x7x365 basis through system-generated
logs, monitoring tools, health reports, ticketing systems, and verification by VMC. In the event that any
centralized infrastructure component fails to meet the prescribed SLA requirements, the applicable penalty shall
be levied on the respective Quarterly O&M/CAMC charges. The Bidder shall restore the affected service within
the stipulated resolution timeline and ensure that surveillance, recording, storage, monitoring, playback, and
video wall display functionalities remain operational.

Any penalty applicable for centralized infrastructure failures shall form part of the overall SLA penalty
calculation. However, the total cumulative SLA penalty recoverable during any quarter, including penalties for
centralized infrastructure and field infrastructure, shall not exceed 10% (Ten Percent) of the Quarterly
O&M/CAMC Invoice Value. Repeated failures of centralized infrastructure affecting surveillance operations
may result in additional contractual actions, including replacement of faulty equipment, invocation of
Performance Security, or termination of the Contract as per the tender conditions. Penalty shall be calculated
directly on the Quarterly O&M Charges as these systems impact the entire surveillance network.

example:

Theft, Vandalism and Damage

       In case of theft, vandalism, accidental damage, fire, pole damage, equipment loss, or any other physical
        damage:
       The Bidder shall immediately lodge complaints/FIRs with the appropriate authorities.
       The Bidder shall coordinate insurance claims.
       The Bidder shall deploy temporary or standby equipment and restore services within SLA timelines.
       Delay in restoration beyond stipulated timelines shall attract SLA penalties.

Fault Rectification Time
     Severity                                        Description                      Resolution Time
    Critical         Pole/Camera/Edge Device completely down                         Within 12 Hours
    Major            Video unavailable, recording failure, Edge Device malfunction   Within 24 Hours
    Minor            Camera angle, focus, cleaning, configuration issue              Within 24 Hours

                                                                                                                     99

Active Video Wall Resolution Time
             Severity                         Incident Type                         Resolution Time
              Critical      Complete Video Wall Failure / Controller Failure        Within 4 Hours
              Major         More than 20% Display Panels Not Functional             Within 8 Hours
              Minor         Single Panel Failure / Display Layout Issue             Within 24 Hours

In the event of failure of the Active Video Wall System, including Video Wall Controller, Video Wall
Management Software, Display Panels, Switching Components, or associated infrastructure, the Bidder shall
restore the services within the stipulated resolution timelines. Failure to comply shall be considered for SLA
calculation and penalty deduction. The penalty shall be calculated and accumulated on a quarterly basis and
deducted from the respective Quarterly O&M/CAMC invoice. The cumulative penalty, including all SLA
parameters under the Project, shall not exceed 10% of the Quarterly O&M/CAMC Invoice Value.

If the Active Video Wall remains unavailable for more than 24 continuous hours or if the same incident occurs
more than three (3) times in a quarter, VMC may direct the Bidder to replace the faulty hardware & software at
no additional cost and may invoke contractual remedies as deemed appropriate.
SLA for Operations & Maintenance (O&M) Services
Service Level Requirement:
The Successful Bidder shall ensure uninterrupted 24x7x365 operation, monitoring, maintenance, fault rectification, and
support of the entire CCTV Surveillance System, including cameras, poles, network, fiber, switches, storage, servers,
VMS, video wall, and associated infrastructure. The Bidder shall deploy adequate resources required to meet the
prescribed SLA requirements throughout the Contract Period.

        Measurement of Service Level Parameters:
        The following shall be considered SLA non-compliances:

          Failure to meet system uptime and availability requirements.
          Failure to meet response and restoration timelines.
          Non-availability of CCTV cameras, network, storage, servers, video wall, VMS, or any associated
           infrastructure beyond permissible limits.
          Failure to provide 24x7 monitoring and technical support services.
          Repeated incidents indicating inadequate resource deployment affecting service levels.
          Failure to submit periodic reports, preventive maintenance records, or incident reports as required by VMC.

    Penalty for Non-Achievement of SLA Requirements:

            Failure to provide 24x7 monitoring and support services                  INR 5,000 per occurrence

5.16 Spare Inventory
           The Successful Bidder shall maintain an adequate inventory of critical spare parts throughout the
           Contract Period to ensure compliance with the prescribed SLA. As a minimum, the Bidder shall
           maintain 5% to 10% spare quantity (or at least one unit, whichever is higher) for all critical
           components supplied under this Contract, including but not limited to CCTV Cameras, PTZ
           Cameras, Switches, SFP Modules, Power Supplies, UPS Batteries, Hard Disks, Media Converters,
           Video Wall LED Display Modules/Panels, Video Wall Controller Modules, Receiving Cards,
           Sending Cards, Power Supply Units, and other critical active components. The spare inventory
           shall be readily available at the project site, CCC, or another location approved by VMC for
           immediate replacement in the event of failure. The cost of maintaining the required spare
           inventory shall be deemed included in the Bid price, and no separate payment shall be made by
           VMC.

5.17 Partial / Full Damage, Theft, and Loss of Hardware
          Hardware installed through this Project would become an important public asset. During the
           contract period of the Project the BIDDER shall be required to repair / replace any equipment
           if stolen / damaged/faulty. Appropriate insurance cover must be provided to all the equipment
           supplied under this project.
          In case of Partial/ Full damage or loss of the equipment due to reasons beyond the control like
           Theft, intentional damage by mishandling, Fire etc., the BIDDER should be in a position to supply
           working standby equipment with same configuration or higher with all services restored, as if it is
           a normal breakdown.
          Service Engineers/ Representatives of BIDDER shall invariably carry their identity cards with
           them, without which they will not be allowed to access department Systems. Service Engineers of
           the BIDDER shall have access to the Computer Systems/ Backup Solution only after
           obtaining clearance from department authorized officials. No component of the System/ data/ log
           information will be taken out of department premises without clearance from authorized Officials
           of the department.

           Hardware, poles, CCTV cameras, edge devices, networking equipment, OFC, switches,

Insurance
   1. The successful Bidder/Contractor shall, at their own cost, obtain and maintain a valid Contractor’s All
      Risk (CAR) Insurance Policy in the joint name of VMC and the Contractor for the entire duration of the
      contract, including any extensions. The policy shall cover all risks of loss or damage to works,
      materials, equipment, machinery, tools, IoT devices, OFC, HDPE pipes, poles, foundations, and
      associated installations from commencement of work until final acceptance by VMC, and shall also
      include third-party liability for bodily injury, death, or property damage arising during execution of the
      work. The sum insured shall not be less than the total contract value and shall cover risks such as fire,
      theft, flood, earthquake, storm, riot, strike, malicious damage, handling and erection risks, and other
      insurable risks relevant to the scope of work. A copy of the valid CAR policy along with proof of
      premium payment shall be submitted to VMC prior to commencement of work, failing which work
      shall not be permitted. In the event of any loss or damage, the Contractor shall immediately notify VMC
      and pursue the insurance claim, and any loss not covered or rejected by the insurer shall be borne
      entirely by the Contractor. Failure to maintain a valid CAR policy throughout the contract period shall
      be treated as a material breach of contract, and VMC shall have the right to suspend or terminate the
      contract without any liability.
   2. The existing goods maintained and new goods supplied & installed under this Contract shall be fully
      insured by the selected bidder, against any loss or damage at the Tenderer’s designated location for
      entire contract period. The insurance taken should cover risks such as flood, theft, fire, earthquake and
      cyclone (for wind speeds up to 80 kmph). The bidder shall submit to the VMC, certificate of insurance
      issued by the insurance company, indicating that such insurances have been taken against entire solution
      & certificate has to be submitted by bidder to VMC. Bidder shall be designated as the 'loss payee' in

                                                                                                                   102

      such insurance policies; bidder shall be liable to pay premium for the insurance policy & shall ensure
      that each & every policy shall keep updated from time to time till end of contract period. In case of
      delayed procurement of insurance from selected bidder, all responsibilities of all supplied and/or
      installed equipment will be on bidder before taking the insurance.
  3. Defect Liability Period (DLP) for the work executed under this contract shall be (60) months from the
     date of final work order by VMC, unless otherwise specified in the tender. During the DLP, the selected
     Bidder/Contractor shall, at its own cost and risk, rectify, repair, or replace any defects, deficiencies,
     damages, or failures in workmanship, materials, equipment, IoT devices, OFC, HDPE pipes, poles,
     foundations, or any part thereof that arise or are noticed during this period, within the time frame
     stipulated by VMC. In case the Contractor fails to attend or rectify such defects within the specified
     time, VMC shall have the right to get the same rectified through a third party at the risk and cost of the
     Contractor, and recover the expenses from the Security Deposit or any other dues payable. The DLP
     obligations shall be in addition to and not in substitution of the warranty obligations under the contract.
  4. The bidder during the term of this contract undertakes to ensure that it has taken or shall take up all
     appropriate insurances for the delivery of goods that it is required to undertake under law as well as to
     adequately cover its obligations under this Contract: shall take out and maintain, at his own cost
     insurance with IRDA approved insurers against the risks, and for the coverage, as specified below: shall
     pay all premium in relation thereto and shall ensure that nothing is done to make such insurance policies
     void or voidable at the Tenderer’s request, shall provide certificate of insurance to the Tenderer
     showing that such insurance has been taken out and maintained. Insurance will include employer's
     liability and workers' compensation insurance in respect of the Personnel of the bidder / bidder’s Team,
     in accordance with the relevant provisions of the Applicable Law, as well as, with respect to such
     Personnel, any such life, health, accident, travel or other insurance as may be appropriate; and Insurance
     against loss of or damage to (i) equipment or assets procured in full or in part for fulfillment of
     obligations under this Contract (ii) the bidder’s assets and property used in the performance of the
     Services (iii) third party insurance for life, health, accident or other insurance as may be appropriate as
     per the applicable law.
  5. In case of theft of any asset, successful bidder(s) will have to carry out all required process to lodge
     police complain / FIR. VMC will assist bidder(s) wherever required.

Review of SLA Parameters

The SLA design is based on the scope of services and operational aspects of Implementation IA. Due to evolving
nature of the proposed project, a review of SLAs will be conducted at the end of three months from the date of
go-live of the Implementation IA. The purpose of this review is to reassess the SLAs based on the first three
months’ call volumes. VMC, after thorough analysis of the monthly statistics monitored as per the above
mentioned SLA parameters, may consider revision of the SLA parameters and update this agreement. And if the
revision occurs, then the revised SLAs would be final and binding for the rest of the term of the contract.

6.1.           Instructions to the bidder
        Bidders are advised to study all instructions, forms, terms, requirements and other information in the
         Bid Documents carefully.

         provided in a separate sheet where required.
        Submission of bid shall be deemed to have been done after careful study and examination of the Bid
         Document with full understanding of its implications.
        The response to this Bid Document should be full and complete in all respects. Failure to furnish all
         information required by the Bid Documents or submission of a proposal not substantially responsive to
         the Bid Documents in every respect will be at the bidder’s risk and may result in rejection of its
         Proposal.
        Additionally, proposals of only those Bidders who satisfy the Conditions of Eligibility, stated herein,
         will be considered for evaluation by VMC.
        Any past projects where the bidder executed other works in addition to what is asked in Pre -
         Qualification Criteria in a single order, the bidder should consider only the value of asked project part
         and highlight only those components.

6.1.1 Purpose of Bid Document
The purpose of this Tender is to select a Bidder for the Implementation of the City Surveillance System in
Vadodara City. This Tender Document provides the information necessary for prospective bidders to understand
the scope, requirements, terms and conditions of the project and to prepare and submit their bids.
Only individual firms/entities meeting the eligibility criteria specified in this Tender Document are permitted to
participate in the bidding process. Consortium, Joint Venture (JV), association of firms, or any similar
arrangement shall not be permitted.
The term “Bidder” shall mean the firm/entity submitting the bid in response to this Tender.
The manner in which the Bid is required to be prepared, submitted, evaluated and accepted is explained in this
Tender Document.

6.1.2 Proposal Preparation Cost
The bidder is responsible for all costs incurred in connection with participation in this process, including, but not
limited to, costs incurred in conduct of informative and other diligence activities, participation in
meetings/discussions/presentations, preparation of proposal, in providing any additional information required by
VMC to facilitate the evaluation process, and in negotiating a definitive Contract or all such activities related to
the bid process. The department will in no case be responsible or liable for those costs, regardless of the conduct
or outcome of the bidding process.
This Bid Document does not commit the VMC to award a contract or to engage in negotiations. Further, no
reimbursable cost may be incurred in anticipation of award. All materials submitted by the Bidder shall become
the property of VMC and may be returned at its sole discretion.

6.1.3 Late Bids
The Bids received after the due date and the specified time (including the extended period if any) shall not be
entertained. The Bids submitted by telex/telegram/fax/e-mail etc. shall not be considered. No correspondence
will be entertained on this matter. PURCHASER shall not be responsible for any delay or non-receipt/ non-
delivery of the documents. No further correspondence on the subject will be entertained. PURCHASER reserves
the right to modify and amend any of the above-stipulated condition/Criteria depending upon project priorities
vis-à-vis urgent commitments.
For the online bid submission, it is advised that the Bidder takes all necessary precaution for the same,
including submitting the Bid well in advance to avoid any last-minute hassles, ensuring that the names/ formats
                                                                                                                        106

of the files to be uploaded are as per the e-tendering portal requirements, using the prescribed browser for
upload etc. PURCHASER shall not entertain any bids which could not be uploaded or uploaded properly in the
portal for whatsoever reasons.

6.1.5 Amendment of RFP Document
At any time before the deadline for submission of bids, the VMC, may, for any reason, whether at its own
initiative or in response to a clarification requested by a prospective Bidder, modify the RFP Document by an
amendment.
The bidders are advised to visit the, https://tender.nprocure.com, www.vmc.gov.in without any notice being
published in the newspaper. Thus, Bidder must check above websites periodically for necessary updates during
tender process VMC also reserves the rights to amend the dates mentioned in this RFP for bid process.
In order to afford prospective Bidders reasonable time in which to take the amendment into account in preparing
their bids, the VMC may, at its discretion, extend the last date for the receipt of Bids.

6.1.6 VMC’s rights to terminate the selection process
VMC may terminate the RFP process at any time and without assigning any reason. VMC makes no
commitments, express or implied, that this process will result in a business transaction with anyone.
This RFP does not constitute an offer by VMC.
The bidder’s participation in this process may result in VMC selecting the bidder to engage in further
discussions and negotiations toward execution of a contract. The commencement of such negotiations does not,
however, signify a commitment by the VMC to execute a contract or to continue negotiations. VMC may
terminate negotiations at any time without assigning any reason.

6.1.7 Right to reject any proposal
Notwithstanding anything contained in this RFP, VMC reserves the right to accept or reject any Proposal and
to annul the Selection Process and reject all Proposals, at any time without any liability or any obligation for
such acceptance, rejection or annulment, and without assigning any reasons therefore.
Besides other conditions and terms highlighted in the Tender Document, bids may be rejected under following
circumstances:

                                                                                                                      107

General Rejection criteria:
 1.       Conditional Bids - If the information provided by the Bidder is found to be incorrect / misleading /
          fraudulent at any stage / time during the Tendering Process;
 2.       Any effort on the part of a Bidder to influence the bid evaluation, bid comparison or contract award
          decisions;
 3.       Bids received after the prescribed time & date for receipt of bids;
 4.       Bids without signature of person (s) duly authorized on required pages of the bid;
 5.       Bids without power of attorney/ board resolution or its certified true copy.
 6.       Technical Rejection criteria
 7.       Bid not sealed and packaged in envelope as asked in RFP;
 8.       Bidders not complying with the Eligibility Criteria given in this Tender
 9.       Technical Bid containing commercial details;
 10.      Revelation of Prices in any form or by any reason before opening the Commercial Bid;
 11.      Failure to furnish all information required by the Tender Document or submission of a Bid not
          substantially responsive to the Tender Document in every respect;
 12.      Bidders not quoting for the complete scope of work as indicated in the Tender Documents, addendum
          /corrigendum (if any) and any subsequent information given to the Bidder;
 13.      Bidders not complying with the Technical and General Terms and conditions as stated in the Tender
          Documents;
 14.      The Bidder not confirming unconditional acceptance of full responsibility of providing services in
          accordance with the scope of work and Service Level Agreements of this Tender;
 15.      Attempt by bidders to influence the decision of bid evaluation committee in unlawful manner or
          proved cause to obstruct the process of fair and transparent bid evaluation by authority.
 16.      During the bid process, if a Bidder indulges in any such deliberate act as would jeopardize or unnecessarily
          delay the process of bid evaluation and finalization.
 17.      During the bid process, if any information found wrong / manipulated / hidden in the bid.

6.1.8 Discount
The Bidders are advised not to indicate any separate discount in the Financial Bid. Discount, if any, should be
merged with the quoted prices. Discount of any type, indicated separately, shall not be considered for evaluation
purpose. However, in the event of such an offer is found to be the lowest without considering the discount, the
Purchaser shall avail such discount at the time of award of contract.

6.1.9    Commercial Rejection Criteria
    1.   Incomplete price Bid;
    2.   Price Bids that do not conform to the Tender’s price bid format;
    3.   Not submitting commercial bid online at https://tender.nprocure.com
    4.   Total price quoted by the Bidder does not include all statutory taxes and levies applicable;
    5.   If there is an arithmetic discrepancy in the commercial Bid calculations the Technical Committee shall
         rectify the same. If the Bidder does not accept the correction of the errors, its Bid may be rejected.
      6. Misrepresentation/ improper response by the Bidder may lead to the disqualification. If such
      7. disqualification / rejection occurs after the Proposals have been opened and the highest ranking Bidder
         gets disqualified / rejected, then VMC reserves the right to consider the next best Bidder, or take any
         other measure as may be deemed fit in the sole discretion of VMC, including annulment of the
         Selection Process.

          Banks except Co-operative Banks (as approved by Gujarat State Govt. and VMC), payable at

        Vadodara. The Bid fees shall be in the form of a Demand Draft.

        Crore) shall be either in form of Demand Draft in favour of “Municipal Commissioner, Vadodara” from
        any nationalized/scheduled banks, payable at Vadodara for the period of 6 years.
        OR
        Bank Guarantee issued by Nationalized Bank including IDBI Bank/Private Banks – Axis Bank,
        HDFC Bank and ICICI Bank along with banks mentioned in the GR of Finance Department of
        Government of Gujarat (FD/MSM/e-file/4/2024/2859/DMO dated 01/05/2025) only in favor of
        “Commissioner, Vadodara Municipal Corporation” for the period of 6 years.
       No interest will be payable by the VMC on the Earnest Money Deposit (EMD).
       In case bid is submitted without EMD or Bid fees as mentioned above then VMC reserves the right to
        reject the bid without providing opportunity for any further correspondence to the bidder concerned.
       The EMD of unsuccessful Bidders will be returned by the Authority, without any Interest, as promptly
        as possible on acceptance of the Proposal of the Selected Bidder or when the Authority cancels the
        Bidding Process.
       The Selected Bidder’s EMD will be returned, without any interest, upon the Selected Bidder signing
        the Agreement and furnishing the Security Deposit / Performance Guarantee in accordance with the
        provision thereof
       The decision of VMC regarding forfeiture of the EMD and rejection of bid shall be final & shall not
        be called upon question under any circumstances.
       The EMD may be forfeited:
             - If a Bidder withdraws their bid or increases their quoted prices during the period of bid
                  validity or its extended period, if any; or
             - In the case of a successful bidder, if the Bidder fails to sign the Contract or to furnish
                  Performance Bank Guarantee within specified time
             - During the bid process, if a Bidder indulges in any such deliberate act as would jeopardize or
                  unnecessarily delay the process of bid evaluation and finalization.
             - During the bid process, if any information found wrong / manipulated / hidden in the bid.
